| 32.1 | Fill-Time Dispersion | REACHED | **Five of five mechanisms built as of 2026-09-19 — the fifth, latency-equalised dispatch, under ADR 0084; the earlier accounting below is kept because its arithmetic error and its wrong obstacle are both worth a reader's time.** Four of five mechanisms built — and the cell's own header said "four" while its body said the remainder was "two mechanisms rather than three". Four plus two is six and the section lists five; the true count was three, and passive-first is what makes it four.** The arithmetic is given because the error was invisible: the header counted the per-venue latency *observation* as the latency-equalised *dispatch* mechanism, and a measurement is not the control it feeds. Both of the two built before this lane are cell-local — which is the property that decided which to build, not a coincidence. Already there: venue-native IOC/FOK selected against the venue's declared capability matrix (`grep -n 'ImmediateOrCancel\|FillOrKill\|fn supports' backend/crates/edge/qip-routing/src/ordertype.rs backend/crates/edge/qip-routing/src/venue.rs`) and a per-venue latency observation feeding a degradation verdict (`grep -n 'latency_multiple_f64\|fn record_ack\|fn observed_latency\|fn assess' backend/crates/edge/qip-routing/src/health.rs`). New: dispersion-aware admission, the pre-trade mechanism that bounds worst-case unwind cost rather than reducing its probability — a cycle whose legs would arrive further apart than the bound is refused whole before it is sent, because a cycle whose legs arrive five milliseconds apart is a position for five milliseconds and the cost of unwinding it is paid at whatever the market did in between. `grep -n 'pub struct DispersionPolicy\|pub enum DispersionVerdict\|pub struct FillTimes\|pub fn assess\|pub fn unmeasured' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/dispersion.rs`, the gate at `grep -n 'GATE_FILL_DISPERSION\|fill_times.observe' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/cell.rs`, reached on the production pass at `grep -n 'confirm_execution_reports\|cell.work' backend/crates/apps/qip-edge-node/src/pass.rs`. The measurement is the cell's own and nothing else's: from the instant it sent an order to the instant the venue's execution report for it was confirmed, taken where both are known. No venue tells the cell its latency, no policy payload carries one, and nothing reads a clock, so a replay of the same reports produces the same verdicts. One exception to the crate's standing discipline is argued rather than assumed: an unmeasured venue does **not** refuse, because a venue has no fill times until it fills something and would fill nothing while refused for having none — a cell that had never traded would then never trade, the discipline closing a loop on itself. What the discipline gets instead is visible silence: `FillTimes::unmeasured` is published on every pass including idle ones as `qip_edge_fill_time_unmeasured_venues`, and a cycle admitted under an unmeasured verdict says so in its journal entry rather than looking like one that passed a check (`a_cell_that_has_filled_nothing_publishes_every_venue_as_unmeasured_rather_than_fast`). Also proven: `a_cycle_whose_two_venues_fill_far_apart_is_refused_whole_on_the_pass_after_it_learns_that`, and `two_venues_that_answer_together_keep_trading_and_their_fill_times_are_published`, which is the half that distinguishes a working gate from one that refuses everything. **New, 2026-09-19 — size decomposition, the fourth mechanism and the second one a cell can hold alone.** The blueprint's row is *"a partial fill at or above the minimum viable fraction completes at reduced size rather than unwinding"*, and the failure it closes was live: `Cell::place_cycle` sent every leg of an admitted cycle at the size the scanner priced, in plan order, whatever the venue had already said about the legs out. A first leg that filled six tenths was followed by a second leg at ten tenths, and the four tenths of difference was an outright position taken at a price chosen for an arbitrage that did not exist at that size — noticed by nothing until the drop copy was reconciled. Unwinding was never the alternative here, because the cell's `Placer` can place and cannot cancel; sizing the rest of the cycle to what the cycle has completed is. `grep -nE 'pub (struct|enum|fn|const) ' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/decomposition.rs` declares `DecompositionPolicy`, `Completion`, `LegSize`, `Decomposition` and `DEFAULT_MINIMUM_VIABLE_FRACTION`; the production caller is `grep -n 'Decomposition::new\|decomposition.size_for\|decomposition.observe' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/cell.rs`, inside `Cell::place_cycle`, reached on the pass through `grep -n 'cell.work(' backend/crates/apps/qip-edge-node/src/pass.rs`. Four properties are load-bearing. **A cycle nothing filled short is sent exactly as it was admitted** — the fraction starts at one and `size_for` returns the planned size unchanged, which is the half that separates a mechanism from a new limit (`a_cycle_whose_venues_fill_every_leg_whole_is_sent_exactly_as_it_was_admitted`). **The fraction only ever falls**, to the worst leg the venues have answered on, because a later leg that filled whole says nothing about the earlier one that did not and a recovering fraction would size a leg above what the cycle in front of it can support. **A leg the venue has said nothing about reduces nothing**, and that distinction is available rather than assumed: `Cell::confirm` breaks on a report of non-positive quantity, so a booked fill is always positive and a zero `filled` is silence. `Placer::execution_reports` defaults to nothing — its own documentation calls that the honest answer for a gateway with no order-entry channel — and reading that default as a zero fill would stop every cycle at every such gateway. The silence is counted instead, on `qip_edge_cycle_legs_total{completion="unanswered"}`, for the same reason `qip_edge_fill_time_unmeasured_venues` exists beside the dispersion gate (`a_cycle_at_a_gateway_that_answers_nothing_is_sent_whole_and_says_so_on_the_series`). **And the reduced size is floored to the venue's lot grid, never rounded up**: a size rounded up is a leg larger than the completed one can support, which is the position the mechanism exists to stop. Flooring here is not the rounding `qip-edge/src/feasibility.rs` refuses — that policy is about an intent a *strategy* handed in, and this size has no such caller; it is the cell's own arithmetic on a fraction the venue reported, journaled as `cycle_decomposed` with the planned size, the size sent and the fraction beside each other. Also proven: `a_cycle_whose_first_leg_fills_three_quarters_sends_its_later_legs_at_three_quarters` (and that the `NetIntent` each decomposed leg is attributed through carries the size sent, not the planned one, because `split_fill` divides a fill across contributors), `a_cycle_whose_first_leg_fills_under_the_minimum_viable_fraction_sends_no_further_leg` (the cell halts, through the `arbitrage_cycle_broken` path that already existed for a cycle stopped between legs — it cannot unwind and says so rather than pretending to), and `a_desk_that_will_only_carry_a_whole_cycle_stops_on_any_short_leg_at_all`, which is what proves the policy is read rather than hard-coded. `DEFAULT_MINIMUM_VIABLE_FRACTION` is a half and is **a stated policy rather than a measured one**, written down as such beside `RateLimits::depletion`'s band multiples so a later measurement can replace it without anybody having to work out whether it was evidence. **New, 2026-09-19 — passive-first on thin venues, the fourth mechanism and the third a cell can hold alone.** The section's row is *"rest on the slow venue, cross the fast ones on its fill"*, and its claimed effect is that this *removes* the slow venue from the exposure window rather than shortening it. The failure it closes is the one size decomposition cannot: decomposition sizes the legs *behind* a short one and can do nothing about the legs already away, so a cycle whose slow leg filled nothing left the fast legs crossed against it and the difference was an outright position at a price chosen for an arbitrage that did not exist. Resting first can, because nothing is away. `grep -nE 'pub (fn|enum) ' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/passive.rs` declares `choose`, `PassiveChoice`, `WholeReason` and `PassiveOutcome`; the production callers are `grep -n 'passive::choose\|fn rest_cycle_leg\|fn resume_rested_cycles' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/cell.rs`, inside `Cell::place_cycle` and `Cell::work`, reached on the pass through `grep -n 'cell.work(' backend/crates/apps/qip-edge-node/src/pass.rs`. Six properties are load-bearing and each is asserted. **The threshold is the operator's own `DispersionPolicy::bound` and not a number this lane invented**: waiting costs a pass and an arbitrage has a shelf life, so a venue is rested on only when its *own* median answer is slower than the operator has said a cycle's legs may be apart. That is not a refinement added for tidiness — the mechanism engaged on any measured difference for exactly one commit and `dispersion.rs`'s `two_venues_that_answer_together_keep_trading_and_their_fill_times_are_published` failed, because its venues are one and two milliseconds apart under a five-millisecond bound. No existing test was changed; the mechanism was. **A cycle whose resting leg fills nothing never becomes a position at all** (`a_resting_leg_that_fills_nothing_is_withdrawn_and_the_near_leg_is_never_crossed`), and the cell is asserted *not* halted there, because nothing went wrong. **A cycle does complete** (`the_near_leg_is_crossed_on_the_pass_the_far_venue_answers_and_not_before`) — the half that separates a mechanism from a new refusal, since a control that rested a leg and never crossed the rest would satisfy every other test here and would have stopped the cell trading cycles altogether. **A leg rests only where the cell could withdraw it**, the same guard `resolve_pricing` puts on a resting net. **A cycle already resting is refused a second admission** under its own gate (`grep -n 'GATE_CYCLE_RESTING' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/cell.rs`), so one opportunity is never opened twice at a venue that has answered neither. **`settle` never retires the order a rested cycle is waiting on**, because that order is the only record of the fraction the remaining legs must be sized to — `grep -n 'awaits_rested_leg' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/cell.rs`. **And a resting leg that half-filled and then expired stops the cell** rather than crossing the remaining legs at a price the market has left, which is the one arm where the mechanism can leave something held. The last two existed and were held by nothing: ten mutations were run against the implementation and those two did not fire, so both got a test (`a_reconciliation_between_passes_never_retires_the_order_a_rested_cycle_is_waiting_on`, `a_resting_leg_that_half_filled_and_then_expired_stops_the_cell_rather_than_crossing_a_stale_price`) and all ten fire now. The region hold commits when the resting leg goes out rather than when the last one does — holds are pass-scoped and `work` sweeps any that outlive their pass — and `commit_cycle_hold` returns what it committed so an abandoned cycle gives back exactly that and nothing recomputed. `cargo test -p qip-edge --test passive` (9 passed, 0 failed); `qip_edge_passive_cycles_total{outcome}` is bounded by two enums, six source-file literals, and the three declining arms are the denominator without which a cell that never rested a leg and a cell that never ran a cycle are one empty series. **What follows was this row's reasoning until 2026-09-19, when ADR 0084 found the obstacle was the wrong one and the mechanism was built the same day; it is kept in the past tense.** Latency-equalised dispatch — the one the section calls the single largest effect — was held **not buildable here**, for two independent reasons. It needs a timer wheel on a dispatch thread this process does not have and ADR 0001/0011 bar adding the runtime it would take; and `Cell` is handed `now` rather than reading a clock, deliberately, so that a replay of the same inputs produces the same orders — which means that within one pass the cell cannot know real elapsed time between two sends at all, and "send at (max − own)" is not a quantity it can compute. Removing the second obstacle would remove replay determinism, so this is a decision rather than a backlog item. `grep -rni 'equalis\|equaliz\|timer_wheel' --include=*.rs backend/crates/edge/ backend/crates/services/qip-execution-engine/` reaches only prose saying so — **and one line of that prose is a trap worth naming**, because it is the shape this register keeps being fooled by. `grep -n 'CrossVenue,' -B 1 backend/crates/edge/qip-routing/src/path.rs` documents §30.2's path 2 as "latency-equalised parallel dispatch within one region", so the phrase exists on a live enum arm that the cell assigns to real cycles. The arm is the path's *name* and the router that assigns it classifies and never routes — it names no venue and produces no order. A reader who greps for the mechanism, finds this, and marks the row built would be reading a label for an implementation. **Re-verified 2026-09-19**: every command in this cell re-run against the tree, all still hold. Everything on the pass reaches no deployed process: `Cell::work` runs only under `QIP_VENUE_FEED=simulated` and `execution_nodes = {}` in all four environments. **Latency-equalised dispatch (mechanism 1) is decided in ADR 0084 (2026-09-19) as an engineering task, not a policy exception**: this row said it needs a dispatch thread this process does not have, and the premise was checked and found to be the wrong obstacle — a timer wheel needs a thread, not an async runtime, and `std::thread` is permitted; the real constraint is replay, and the design meets it by having the cell compute a release schedule as a pure function of the pass from the per-venue fill-time median it already keeps (`grep -n 'pub fn median' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/dispersion.rs`), carried through the `at` that `Placer::place` already takes (`grep -n 'gateway.place(' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/cell.rs`), with the fill time measured from the release instant so the equaliser cannot chase its own tail. **Built 2026-09-19, and the sentence before this one was true until that day.** `grep -n 'pub fn release_schedule\|pub struct ReleaseSchedule' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/dispersion.rs` declares the schedule — offsets `max_median − median(venue)` over a cycle's venues, a pure function of the fill-time window, so a replay computes the same one; an unmeasured venue makes it unequalised with every offset zero and is named rather than refused, for the reason the gate beside it gives. `grep -n 'release_schedule(' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/cell.rs` is the production reader: once per whole cycle in `place_cycle`, over the crossed legs in `resume_rested_cycle`, and the single-venue schedule for a net and for a resting leg, computed rather than assumed so every `order_sent` entry carries the same two fields. `Placer::place`'s `at` now means "release no earlier than", `OpenOrder` carries `release_at` beside `sent_at`, `Decision::OrderSent` carries `release_at` and `equalised` (skipped when absent so every journal sealed before the fields still verifies), and **the fill time is measured from `release_at`** — `grep -n 'let release_at = working.order.release_at' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/cell.rs` — which is the detail the record says decides whether the mechanism works, and the mutation that measured from `sent_at` instead failed `the_slowest_venue_is_released_first_and_every_leg_arrives_together` with the near venue's median at thirty milliseconds against one: the equaliser chasing its own tail, exactly as predicted. The simulated gateway honours the instant on the pass: `grep -n 'pub fn advance_to\|fn unreleased\|if at > self.now' backend/crates/apps/qip-edge-node/src/gateway.rs` holds an order placed for an instant past the pass it is on, releases it on the first pass that reaches it (`run_pass` advances the gateway before anything else reads the venue — `grep -n 'gateway.advance_to' backend/crates/apps/qip-edge-node/src/pass.rs`), and withdraws rather than sends late one whose instant a pass finds further past than `DEFAULT_RELEASE_TOLERANCE`, reporting it through the new `Placer::unreleased`; the cell books that under `GATE_RELEASE_LATE` — a `pub const`, recorded directly because there is no `WorkReport` on that path, so `grep -n 'metrics\.refusal(' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/cell.rs` printed **five** lines on 2026-09-19 evening where the observability rule file says four; the fifth passes a constant and the label set is still bounded — closes the order, returns what the region committed, and halts, because its chain says sent and the venue never saw it. Proven in `cargo test -p qip-edge --test release` (arrival together, replay identity across two cells with a non-zero offset in play, the withdrawal), `--lib dispersion` (the arithmetic, the unmeasured case, the single-venue case) and `cargo test -p qip-edge-node --test gateway` (held and not released a millisecond short, released on the instant, withdrawn ten seconds late, a backwards pass refused); every one mutation-verified. **Four honest limits, and they are why `REACHED` here means built and reached rather than moving any number.** First, every offset is zero on the simulated arm — but not for the reason ADR 0084 gave. Its context said the simulator "matches in-process with no latency model"; `grep -n 'latency: Duration::from_millis' backend/crates/services/qip-brokers/src/exchange.rs` shows a fixed four-millisecond latency on every fill. The offsets are zero because a node reaches **one** venue and equalisation is a difference between venues, not because fills are instantaneous. Second, the simulator can release a held order only on a pass, so a node whose passes are further apart than the tolerance will withdraw every held leg and halt on the first the day a latency model makes an offset non-zero; `DEFAULT_RELEASE_TOLERANCE`'s doc says so. Third, ADR 0084 §4's release thread for a real gateway is not built, and `RestGateway::place` still releases immediately; that is not wrong today because `run_pass` is typed to the simulated gateway and a live gateway is refused at start-up, and its comment says as much. `qip_edge_release_lag_nanos` therefore has no recording site and is not described, per the observability rule. Fourth, nothing is deployed: `execution_nodes = {}` in every environment. |

| 34.1 | What an Adapter Must Provide | PARTIAL | **Seven of nine provisions built, and the count itself is corrected here.** This row read "Five of nine" while listing six, because the reconciliation reader was named after the word "plus" and never counted; the register has been wrong in the optimistic direction before, and this one was wrong in the other, which is worth saying out loud because an understated row invites a lane to rebuild something that exists. The nine are protocol binding, fee schedule, order-type matrix, settlement rules, withdrawal policy, rate limits, latency profile, minimums/tick/lot, and reconciliation endpoint. **Fee schedule, corrected 2026-09-19.** This row counted the provision built because `VenueProfile` declares a tiered schedule with a maker/taker split. It declared one and nothing charged one: `grep -rn 'FeeSchedule::tiered\|VenueProfile::listed' backend/crates --include=*.rs` was test-only in both forms, so the provision was a type rather than a behaviour — the understating direction this row was already corrected for once, in a new place. It is now charged: `qip-brokers`' `SimulatedExchange` holds a `FeeSchedule`, keeps its own tally of what the account has traded there, and bills each fill at the rung that tally selects on the side of the liquidity it supplied, refusing rather than booking free when a rate cannot be applied (`cargo test -p qip-brokers --test fees`, six tests, all mutation-verified). `VenueProfile` itself still has no production constructor and the `Router` that consumes one is still unreached, so the router-side half of this provision is unchanged. Built: protocol binding (`grep -n 'pub trait' backend/crates/services/qip-brokers/src/adapter.rs`), a tiered fee schedule with a maker/taker split, the order-type capability matrix, minimums/tick/lot, and a measured latency profile — `sed -n '/^pub struct VenueProfile/,/^}/p' backend/crates/edge/qip-routing/src/venue.rs` — a reconciliation reader for balances (`grep -n 'fn reconcile\|struct HoldingObservation' backend/crates/services/qip-capital-fabric/src/wallet.rs`), and **per-venue order and message rate limits**, which are new. The limits are declared on the venue profile — there is no `Option` and no unlimited arm, so a venue that publishes nothing gets `RateLimits::ASSUMED` rather than no control at all (`sed -n '/pub const ASSUMED/,/};/p' backend/crates/edge/qip-routing/src/ratelimit.rs`) — and they are enforced at two independent seams that count separately on purpose. The router drops a venue whose window is spent **before an order object exists** (`grep -c 'ExclusionReason::RateLimited' backend/crates/edge/qip-routing/src/router.rs` prints 1), and the venue itself refuses the order that exceeds its own allowance while never refusing a cancel (`grep -c 'spend_order\|record_message' backend/crates/services/qip-brokers/src/exchange.rs` prints 3: the submit refusal, and the cancel and replace message counts). Proven to fire rather than declared: `cargo test -p qip-routing -p qip-brokers` runs `a_venue_whose_order_allowance_is_spent_is_dropped_from_the_comparison_with_a_reason`, `the_allowance_is_read_from_the_profile_so_two_venues_do_not_share_one_budget`, `the_venue_refuses_the_order_that_exceeds_its_published_allowance_and_admits_it_a_window_later` and `a_cancel_is_never_refused_by_the_rate_limit_and_is_still_counted_against_it`. The venue seam is on a non-test path: `grep -c 'exchange.submit' backend/crates/apps/qip-edge-node/src/gateway.rs` prints 2, so a binary reaches it — though `grep -rn 'execution_nodes = {}' infrastructure/environments/*/terraform.tfvars` matches every environment — this read "prints four lines" until 2026-09-19, when the command printed five without the claim becoming false, so the figure is replaced by the predicate — so no deployed process does. The router seam reaches no caller outside `qip-routing`'s own tests, which was already true of `Router::route` before this work: `grep -rn 'qip_routing::router\|qip_routing::Router' --include=*.rs backend/crates \| grep -v '^backend/crates/edge/qip-routing/'` prints one line, and it is a string literal in an acceptance test's module list rather than a call. **Still absent, and both blocked on a seam outside `qip-brokers` rather than on effort.** Withdrawal policy: ADR 0021 permits the deterministic half and refuses the signing half, and ADR 0051 already models the venue's own allowlist as configured out of band and mirrored by `CustodyPolicy::mirrors_the_venue_allowlist` in `qip-capital-fabric`; the consumer the blueprint names for this provision is the corridor and custody model, which is §37 and that crate. A second allowlist in `qip-brokers` would be a second source of truth for a fact ADR 0051 already places elsewhere. Settlement rules joined to a per-venue jurisdiction calendar: the calendar exists and is not duplicable — `grep -n 'pub struct SettlementCalendar' backend/crates/services/qip-capital-fabric/src/settlement.rs` — but it lives in a service, and `.claude/rules/architecture/00-boundaries.md` says a service exposes its domain through types rather than reaching into another service. Closing this wants `SettlementCalendar` moved into `qip-financial`, where `MarketHours` already is and which both crates already depend on; that move touches `qip-capital-fabric`. `grep -rni 'withdrawal_policy' --include=*.rs backend/crates/edge/qip-routing/ backend/crates/services/qip-brokers/` still returns nothing. **The settlement provision was re-judged 2026-09-19 with both crates in one lane's hands, and the blocker this row named is not the real one.** The objection recorded here was a crate boundary — `SettlementCalendar` living in a service — and the proposed remedy was moving it into `qip-financial`. That move is defensible on its own terms and the argument should be recorded: a *move* is not a reach, `qip-financial` is a lib with no I/O that already holds `MarketHours` and `TradingCalendar`, and both crates already depend on it. **It closes nothing.** The provision is "stage and cycle, joined to the settlement calendar for **this venue's jurisdiction**", and the join is what is missing, not the residence. `grep -rn 'SettlementCalendar' --include=*.rs backend/crates` finds exactly one production construction, `SettlementCalendar::weekday(SettlementConvention::T1)` inside `Platform::new` (locate it with `grep -n 'SettlementCalendar::' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs`), and it is **one global T+1 weekday calendar for every venue** — no jurisdiction is consulted because the planner holds a single calendar, not a book of them. Making it per venue means changing where that calendar is selected, which is `qip-kernel`, and making the adapter *provide* stage and cycle means `qip-brokers` and `qip-routing`'s `VenueProfile`. Both are outside this lane. Moving the type without either would produce a per-jurisdiction book nothing keys into — a control that cannot fire — so the move was declined rather than made half. Withdrawal policy is unchanged and still blocked by ADR 0021 and ADR 0051 as recorded above. **Seven of nine stands.** — **The kernel half of the settlement provision was built 2026-09-19, and the previous entry's blocker is corrected: the join did not need the type moved.** `SettlementBook` sits beside `SettlementCalendar` in `qip-capital-fabric` (`grep -n 'pub struct SettlementBook' backend/crates/services/qip-capital-fabric/src/settlement.rs`): calendars declared per jurisdiction — `Region`, the type `location.rs` already called "the jurisdiction whose calendar and funding curve apply" — and venues assigned to one, refusing a venue in an undeclared jurisdiction, a second calendar for one jurisdiction, and a venue assigned twice. `PrePositioningPlanner` holds the book instead of one calendar and asks it per lane (`grep -n 'calendar_for' backend/crates/services/qip-capital-fabric/src/plan.rs`): a lane at a venue with no jurisdiction, or keyed on a jurisdiction its venue does not settle in, is refused as `unpriceable` with the venue named and the act that clears it, while the declared lanes beside it are still planned — never quoted on a default. `Platform::new` declares the home jurisdiction on the T+1 weekday calendar and assigns exactly the desk broker's venue to it (`grep -n 'SettlementBook::new\|assign_venue' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs`), so the one global calendar this row named is gone and `grep -rn 'SettlementCalendar::weekday' --include=*.rs backend/crates/runtime` finds it only inside a `declare_jurisdiction`. Proven: `cargo test -p qip-capital-fabric --test capital_fabric each_lane_is_quoted_on_its_own_venues_jurisdiction_calendar` (a T+0 venue and a T+2 venue with one Friday deadline — one moved, one refused as settling too late, in the same plan), `a_lane_at_a_venue_with_no_declared_jurisdiction_is_refused_by_name`, `a_lane_keyed_on_a_jurisdiction_its_venue_does_not_settle_in_is_refused`, and in the kernel `cargo test -p qip-kernel --test platform_outcomes the_desk_venue_settles_on_its_jurisdictions_calendar` (the book names the desk venue under `home` and nothing else; a lane the forecaster raised at `XTKS` is refused by name while the desk's is quoted). **What the adapter side must still supply, and this is the other half of the provision rather than a detail:** `VenueProfile` carries no settlement convention and no jurisdiction, so the venue-admission seam in the kernel has nothing to call `assign_venue` with; until `qip-routing`'s profile (or the `qip-brokers` adapter it is built from) declares `(SettlementConvention, jurisdiction)` per venue and the kernel's admission path declares and assigns from it, every venue but the desk's is refused by name in the plan. That refusal is the provision fail-closed, not the provision built. **Seven of nine stands; the settlement provision is half, and the half that exists refuses rather than defaults.** |

(2) The node's simulated venue lives in the process and dies with it, so after a real restart both sides of the account comparison are empty — the arm that finds an order the dead session left is exercised in test, and a gateway to a venue that does outlive the cell has no account channel, so such a node stays paused (fail closed, asserted by `a_restarted_node_whose_second_venue_cannot_answer_keeps_refusing_rather_than_resuming`). (3) The region wire rides the halt flag's mount and no Terraform names it, so a deployment without `QIP_HALT_FLAG_PATH` can never be told a peer is dark; the node prints which of the two it is at start-up. **What is left is one column of one row**: §36.3's cloud-region row also asks that global exposure be recomputed without the dark region, which is the central plane's. **Examined by the kernel lane on 2026-09-19 and deliberately not built; the reason is a blocker rather than a backlog item.** The centre holds no input that says a region is dark. `grep -n 'pub struct CellReport' -A 40 backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/central/plane.rs` carries positions, utilisation, breaks, orders, fills, crosses and refusals and nothing about a peer being unreachable; `RegionOutlook` and the region wire are `qip-edge`'s and `qip-edge-node`'s (`grep -n 'pub enum RegionOutlook' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/region.rs`); and `RegionMembership` is an argument the plane is handed per call, not state it keeps (`grep -n 'membership: &RegionMembership' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/central/plane.rs`). Every route to giving the centre that input crosses a boundary the kernel lane does not own — the wire contract, the edge, or a composition root. **And the plane-side recompute alone would be a control that can only loosen, reachable by nothing.** The aggregate is rebuilt in `ingest` (`grep -n 'AggregateExposure::of' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/central/plane.rs`) and feeds `concentrations` and `crowded`, which feed `recall_for`; dropping a dark region's last-known book lowers the cell count `crowded` needs and moves every bucket's share, so it can withdraw a recall that would otherwise have issued. Adding that with no caller is the `MaxExpectedShortfall` shape inverted — not a control that cannot fire, but a relaxation nothing can audit. So: five rows reached, one row reached on the cells' side, and the centre's column recorded as blocked on an input that does not exist rather than on code nobody has written. **Re-verified 2026-09-19 from the edge side, and the blocker is larger than "the kernel lane does not own the wire" — it is a four-crate chain crossing three ownership boundaries, and no one owner can close it.** Trace it rather than taking this on trust. The cell has the fact and publishes an accessor for it: `grep -n 'pub const fn region_outlook\|pub fn is_region_dark' backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/cell.rs`. The fact leaves the cell only in `CellStateDelta` (`grep -n 'pub struct CellStateDelta' -A 55 backend/crates/edge/qip-edge/src/mesh.rs`), which carries no field for it. That type is mirrored **field for field** by a second declaration the compiler cannot hold to it — `grep -n 'struct WireDelta' -A 22 backend/crates/services/qip-mesh/src/delta.rs`, and the module doc says why there are two — so a field added on the edge side alone is decoded by nothing and dropped one hop later, which is the worst of the three outcomes: a value computed and ignored that reads on inspection like a control. From `WireDelta` the centre's view is built in an app, `grep -n 'fn report_from' backend/crates/apps/qip-api/src/mesh.rs`, and only there does it become the kernel's `CellReport`. Four crates — `qip-edge`, `qip-mesh`, `qip-api`, `qip-kernel` — and a lane owning any one of them can build a half that nothing reads. **And the kernel lane's second objection is untouched by supplying the input**: the plane-side recompute can only loosen, because dropping a dark region's last-known book lowers the cell count `crowded` needs and can withdraw a recall that would otherwise have issued. That is a question about what the centre should *do* with the knowledge, and it has to be answered before the wire is worth widening. Recorded as blocked on a decision plus a four-crate change, which is an ADR-shaped problem and not a lane-shaped one. The five rows and the cells' half of the sixth are unchanged and every command in this cell still holds. **Decided 2026-09-19 in ADR 0079, awaiting implementation.** The dark-region arm was "needs an ADR"; it is decided, and one premise handed to the lane was false: `grep -rn 'region_outlook\|is_region_dark' --include=*.rs backend/crates/` prints nothing, so no cell publishes the fact — and a crashed node could not. ADR 0079 makes "dark" the centre's derivation from silence past `CentralConfig::region_dark_after`, carried to the plane on a defaulted `CellReport::region` filled from the `region` the delta already carries (`grep -n 'fn report_from' backend/crates/apps/qip-api/src/mesh.rs`) so the doubly-declared delta gains no field; holds the last book in the aggregate rather than dropping it, because `grep -n 'pub fn crowded' -A 8 backend/crates/services/qip-capital/src/exposure.rs` and `grep -n 'fn cells_behind' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/central/plane.rs` show that dropping it lowers the cell count and withdraws a recall — row three's literal reading can only loosen and is refused; refuses new grants in `issue` and freezes the region's share; and suspends mirrors through a new subtract-only `FeasibilityConstraints::dark_regions` read at `check_extension_for` against ADR 0073's `venue_regions`. This row moves when a lane lands the four-crate change and the monotonicity test ADR 0079 names. **Landed 2026-09-19 (lane K1): ADR 0079 is implemented across all four crates, and the verdict stays `PARTIAL` for one named reason — the window that arms the derivation is stated by no composition root.** One premise in the ADR's own context is false in this tree and is corrected here rather than carried: it says `grep -rn 'region_outlook\|is_region_dark' --include=*.rs backend/crates/` prints nothing; it prints the cell's `apply_region_outlook`/`is_region_dark` and their callers, which the region-wire lane landed. | 38.3 | Reconciliation | PARTIAL | The reached half stands — the exact arithmetic, the per-venue-asset halt, surplus treated as shortfall, and no path that writes a correction, driven from LEARN each cycle: `grep -n 'fn reconcile\|ReconciliationOutcome::Halt\|BreakCause::DeltaBeyondTolerance' backend/crates/services/qip-capital-fabric/src/wallet.rs` and `grep -n 'WalletCommand::Reconcile' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs`. **The tolerance is now §38.3's formula rather than the constant the platform shipped**: `tolerance = dust + rate × |expected|`, per **venue-asset**, with the section's class beside it — `grep -n 'pub enum ToleranceClass\|pub struct ToleranceBasis\|pub fn dust_only\|pub fn evaluate\|pub struct ToleranceSchedule' backend/crates/services/qip-capital-fabric/src/tolerance.rs`. Three silent failures close with it. A constant cannot be right for two books — a dollar of drift on a hundred dollars is a break, a dollar on a hundred million is a rounding artefact — so whichever book the number was chosen for, the other was mis-judged, and the too-loose direction is the `MaxExpectedShortfall` defect with the sign reversed (`the_same_basis_allows_more_on_a_larger_book_and_less_on_a_smaller_one`, `a_tolerance_that_the_formula_widened_still_halts_beyond_it_and_not_inside`). It was keyed by asset, so a statement for a second venue holding USD silently replaced the first venue's control, and which venue ended up judged by a tolerance nobody there set depended on the order statements arrived in (`two_venues_holding_one_asset_are_judged_by_their_own_floors`, `two_venues_holding_dollars_keep_their_own_floors_through_the_learn_stage`). And it carried no class, so a halt could not say which of the section's six rules it had applied; every outcome now carries the evaluated formula with all six terms, so the figure behind a halt is re-derivable from the record rather than trusted. The tolerance decides whether to **halt** on a disagreement and no longer whether the disagreement happened: a non-zero delta inside tolerance is `WithinTolerance` carrying its direction, `Reconciled` now means the two claims agreed exactly, and §38.3's "a persistent non-zero delta inside tolerance is a modelling defect and opens a ticket" is a per-venue-asset streak that extends only while the divergence keeps leaning the same way, restarts on a flip and clears on an exact agreement or a halt — `grep -n 'fn drift_tickets\|DriftStreak' backend/crates/services/qip-capital-fabric/src/journal.rs`, with `a_delta_that_keeps_leaning_the_same_way_inside_tolerance_opens_a_ticket`, `a_divergence_that_changes_direction_restarts_the_streak_rather_than_extending_it` and `an_exact_agreement_or_a_halt_clears_the_drift_a_venue_asset_had_accumulated`. Two silences close beside it: reconciling a wallet holding no venue-asset answered `Ok(vec![])`, byte-identical to the answer for a book that reconciled perfectly and the answer a deployment gives for as long as no statement arrives, and it now refuses and says so (`an_empty_wallet_refuses_to_reconcile_rather_than_reporting_a_clean_book`); an empty schedule reports `Idle` with its reason rather than being an absence a caller must know to ask about (`an_empty_schedule_says_it_is_idle_rather_than_reading_as_permissive`). **Short of `REACHED`, and the gap is now narrower and differently shaped than this row said.** It read "no funding rate, deposit rate or mark interval exists anywhere in this platform"; a **deposit rate now does**. The euro area's deposit facility rate is fetched from the ECB's own data portal by the `ecb-key-interest-rates` connector, which clears the same licensing gate the Frankfurter source does before any transport is built — licence `ecb-website-copyright-free-use`, class `Public`, keyless — `grep -n 'ecb-key-interest-rates' backend/crates/services/qip-data-finder/src/admission.rs backend/crates/services/qip-data-finder/src/registration.rs backend/crates/services/qip-market-ingestion/src/connector_feed.rs` and `the_ecb_key_interest_rates_are_admitted_for_trade_under_the_ecb_s_own_terms`. It arrives as a macro observation stamped with the date it applied to and withheld until sixteen hours later (`the_recorded_message_decodes_into_one_level_per_key_rate_with_the_ecb_s_own_figures`, `a_key_rate_is_withheld_until_the_ecb_would_have_published_the_day_it_applies_to`, `the_same_message_served_twice_releases_its_levels_once` in `backend/crates/services/qip-market-ingestion/tests/ecb_key_rates.rs`), and `Platform::observe` records it as one day's accrual on the ECB's own actual/360 convention — `grep -n 'absorb_interval_rate\|interval_rate_series\|policy_rates' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs` is the production call path, `the_ecb_deposit_facility_rate_absorbed_at_sense_becomes_a_38_3_interval_rate` drives it. **A funding rate and a mark interval still do not exist**, and `SourcedIntervalRate::from_percent_per_annum` refuses to be the one that supplies them: an annual percentage is one day without a second assumption and is a funding interval only with one (`a_per_annum_figure_is_refused_for_every_class_the_section_does_not_measure_in_days`). **The desk's dollar cash book now carries a real sourced rate, and this row said it could not.** It read "no production basis carries a non-zero rate yet" on the reasoning that an issuer sets a rate for a currency, that the one class this process can attest is the desk's own cash at its broker, and that the book is in dollars while the only admitted rate was the euro's. Every clause of that was true and the conclusion has been closed rather than argued away: the **effective federal funds rate** is fetched from the Federal Reserve Bank of New York's own markets API by the `nyfed-effr` connector, through the *unmodified* admission gate — licence `nyfed-terms-of-use-reference-rates`, class `Public`, keyless, `Redistribute` included — `grep -n 'nyfed-effr' backend/crates/services/qip-data-finder/src/admission.rs backend/crates/services/qip-data-finder/src/registration.rs backend/crates/services/qip-market-ingestion/src/connector_feed.rs` and `the_new_york_feds_effective_federal_funds_rate_is_admitted_for_trade_under_its_terms_of_use`. It arrives as a macro observation stamped with the date its transactions took place and withheld five days — a single scalar covering the worst case the recording itself shows, a rate for the Friday before a Monday holiday not published until the Tuesday (`the_recorded_response_decodes_into_one_rate_per_business_day_with_the_new_york_feds_own_figures`, `an_effective_rate_is_withheld_until_the_new_york_fed_would_have_published_the_day_it_covers`, `the_rates_are_published_oldest_first_however_the_vendor_ordered_its_array`, `the_same_response_served_twice_releases_its_rates_once` in `backend/crates/services/qip-market-ingestion/tests/nyfed_effr.rs`). `Platform::observe` records it as one day's accrual on the US money market's own actual/360 convention — checked against that market rather than copied from the euro arm, and the two coincide because two money markets settled on the same convention. The production call path is `grep -n 'POLICY_RATE.US.EFFR\|absorb_interval_rate\|interval_rate_series' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs`; `the_effective_federal_funds_rate_absorbed_at_sense_becomes_a_38_3_interval_rate_for_dollars` and `the_learn_stage_judges_the_desks_dollar_cash_by_a_published_rate_rather_than_its_dust_floor` drive it end to end, the second asserting the LEARN stage's basis carries `0.000100833` rather than zero. **The separation the euro lane refused to breach still holds, by currency and not by which feed shipped**: EFFR answers for a dollar book and `NoneHeld` for every other, the deposit facility rate answers for a euro book and `NoneHeld` for every other, and a sterling book is still at its dust floor (`a_dollar_rate_and_a_euro_rate_are_held_apart_and_neither_answers_for_the_other_s_book`, `a_euro_rate_on_its_own_leaves_the_desks_dollar_cash_at_its_dust_floor_and_the_record_says_why`, `a_euro_rate_never_answers_for_a_book_in_another_currency`). **The New York Fed's terms attach an obligation this platform can carry and cannot discharge.** Their Use Restrictions require a named notice and disclaimer with any *presentation* of reference rate data; the completed text is `NyFedEffrConnector::REFERENCE_RATE_NOTICE`, the kernel's series table forces every arm to name a notice or to name that there is none, and `SourcedIntervalRate::presentation_notice` carries it into the derivation sentence `Platform::tolerance_reasons` keeps beside the basis (`a_tolerance_derived_from_the_effective_rate_carries_the_notice_the_new_york_fed_demands`, `the_reference_rate_notice_the_terms_require_is_carried_verbatim_by_the_connector`). Carrying it is a mechanism; **rendering it is the obligation, and nothing renders this rate today — whoever first does owns it**, as the catalogue entry says in the same words the ECB entries use about acknowledgement. The attribution format, the same-permissions condition on redistribution, and the Terms' own instruction that users monitor the site for changes are likewise recorded and unenforceable by any check in the tree. What the record gains instead is the **cause**: `rate: 0` reads identically whether nobody publishes for the currency, the rate held is for another row, the publisher has gone quiet or the class accrues nothing, and `RateLookup` makes those four separate findings that `Platform::tolerance_reasons` keeps beside each basis (`grep -n 'pub enum RateLookup\|pub struct SourcedIntervalRate\|pub struct PolicyRateTable\|pub fn from_sourced' backend/crates/services/qip-capital-fabric/src/tolerance.rs`, with `a_published_deposit_rate_becomes_one_days_accrual_and_widens_only_by_that`, `a_rate_knowable_before_it_was_true_is_refused_as_point_in_time_leakage`, `a_rate_whose_publisher_has_gone_quiet_stops_governing_and_the_dust_floor_returns`, `a_superseded_rate_offered_after_a_later_one_is_refused_rather_than_taken` and `a_sourced_rate_faces_the_same_ceiling_a_declared_one_does` in `backend/crates/services/qip-capital-fabric/tests/sourced_tolerance.rs`). **Nothing is deployed and nothing is scraped**: neither `data-api.ecb.europa.eu` nor `markets.newyorkfed.org` is in the Envoy bootstrap or in `egress_allowed_upstreams`, so a deployment cannot reach either source until that separate infrastructure step is taken under ADR 0034 — the same position the ADR 0034 candidates are in, and stated here rather than inferred. Both connectors are proven against recorded bodies and against nothing a deployed process has fetched; a connector is not a feed. `grep -n 'class_for_desk_cash\|ToleranceBasis::dust_only\|ToleranceBasis::from_sourced' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs` is the seam, and it shows the one class this process can attest: the desk's own cash at its broker is fiat at a broker and everything a statement otherwise names is `Undeclared`, because reading a class out of an asset string — "BTC looks like crypto spot" — would put a §38.3 row nobody chose behind a halt (`the_learn_stage_judges_the_desks_cash_by_the_38_3_row_it_can_attest`, `only_the_desks_own_cash_is_classified_without_being_told`). Five of the six classes therefore have a rule and no data, and the sixth now has data for the currency this deployment's book is actually in as well as for one it is not. That the rate arm is a working gate rather than one that refuses everything is `an_interval_rate_that_could_never_halt_is_refused_and_a_usable_one_is_admitted`; `a_class_that_38_3_gives_no_accrual_to_cannot_be_given_an_interval_rate` refuses an operator's number the formula would have ignored. `FabricRecord::SCHEMA_VERSION` goes 3 to 4 and a version-3 reconciliation is refused by version rather than re-judged, because the hash chain proves a record has not changed, not that the rule that produced it still holds. |

Strategy marketplace — partial: `/strategies` carries id, cell, venue, stage and `holds_capital`, no evidence, capacity or correlation against holdings, and not even the family (`awk '/^fn strategies\(/{f=1} f&&/"family"/{print} f&&/^}$/{exit}' backend/crates/apps/qip-api/src/routes.rs` prints nothing while `grep -n 'pub fn family' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/central/factory.rs` shows the factory holds it — the one-line seam is §40.11's). The fourth is half for the reason this row already gives (per rule, not per declined order — the page says so beside the regret it shows), and the seventh is half because cost is served (`GET /models` `cost_micros`) and attributed return is not (`grep -n 'NO_ATTRIBUTION' backend/crates/apps/qip-api/src/routes.rs` is `/pnl`'s one arm); the page renders that sentence verbatim and does not divide a figure the platform sent by one it did not. `npx playwright test tests/intelligence-explanations.spec.ts --project=desktop-chromium` — **2 passed**; five mutations fired on the property, each after a rebuild: the seven rendered reversed failed the DOM-order deep-equality; the refused-estimate filter inverted failed `Expected "1" Received "2"`; coverage inferred from a live outcome failed `Expected "1" Received "0"` on the nothing-answered test; the simulated flag dropped failed `Expected substring: "simulated"`; `/pnl` paraphrased failed on the platform's sentence. | 40.3 | Identity and Access | PARTIAL | Roles, devices and step-up are modelled in the shared auth package: `grep -n 'AuthMethod\|MfaMethod\|reauthenticate' frontend/packages/auth/src/index.ts`. But §40.3's load-bearing claim — passkeys, "no passwords anywhere" — is contradicted by the same line: `passkey` is one arm of a union whose first arm is `password`, and password routes are shipped: `ls frontend/portal/src/app/api/auth` shows `forgot-password` and `reset-password`. No WebAuthn ceremony or attestation code exists: `grep -rn 'navigator.credentials\|attestationObject' frontend/portal/src` returns nothing. **Amended 2026-09-19 (ADR 0038 accepted with conditions).** All three evidence commands above were re-run in the deciding lane's worktree and every one still holds: `AuthMethod` is still `"password" \| "google" \| "passkey" \| "saml" \| "oidc" \| "development"`, the two password routes are still shipped, and the ceremony search still returns nothing — so the verdict does not move. What moved is the decision: passkeys as the only credential, the sealed-cookie session, the retirement of the password routes, the recovery discipline (a second passkey, or an operator-issued single-use **single-attempt** code that opens only enrolment) and every refusal are accepted; which surface verifies the passkey is a decision table with one activation step, a `passkeyEnrollment:start` probe against the dev project whose HTTP status and code select the row, **not yet run** — the lane had no web access (four tool errors quoted in the ADR), no shell and no credential. The brief for both implementing lanes is `docs/architecture/adr-0038-implementation-brief.md`. Two honest limits, stated so this row is not read as narrower than §40.3 or wider than the ADR: the section's *recovery* row ("a mandatory delay, every registered contact notified") and its *devices* row's "with attestation" are **not** covered by ADR 0038 and are named as open in the brief's §5; and the platform side gains only a `Presence::Attested` variant (`grep -n 'pub enum Presence' -A 8 backend/crates/apps/qip-api/src/auth.rs` shows the single `Unattested` variant and the doc comment naming the second), which is downstream of ADR 0042, itself proposed and unapplied. **Later on 2026-09-19 the orchestrating session answered check 1's documentation half from the primary source** (`curl -sS 'https://identitytoolkit.googleapis.com/$discovery/rest?version=v2' | grep -ci passkey` printed 0 at revision 20260911, and the same for v1), while the gateway routes the four methods; ADR 0038's third row is selected — stay on the password path, no fall-through — so this row is short of REACHED on a Google documentation event rather than on any work in this tree, and the shape-independent work in `docs/architecture/adr-0038-implementation-brief.md` §2.1 is the part a lane may take. |

Every other row's authentication column is unbuilt: `grep -rn 'biometric\|enclave' frontend/portal/src` returns nothing, and there is no withdraw, second-approval, or hardware-key path on either surface. **Amended 2026-09-19 (ADR 0038 accepted with conditions).** Re-run in the deciding lane's worktree: `/orders` is still `Method::Get`, `Role::Viewer` (`grep -n 'pattern: "/orders"' -B 2 -A 3 backend/crates/apps/qip-api/src/routes.rs`), and the `biometric\|enclave` search still returns nothing, so the verdict does not move. The ADR added that as a reversal condition on 2026-09-19 and the brief's §4 names the three paper-trading layers it leaves untouched. "Biometric" as §40.4 writes it — a platform authenticator with user verification — is what a passkey ceremony with `userVerification: required` *is*; the row will read it as built only when the ceremony exists and a Playwright virtual authenticator with `hasUserVerification` drives it. The web-only capabilities the section reserves for a hardware key are unchanged and unbuilt. **Later on 2026-09-19 the orchestrating session answered check 1's documentation half from the primary source** (`curl -sS 'https://identitytoolkit.googleapis.com/$discovery/rest?version=v2' | grep -ci passkey` printed 0 at revision 20260911, and the same for v1), while the gateway routes the four methods; ADR 0038's third row is selected — stay on the password path, no fall-through — so this row is short of REACHED on a Google documentation event rather than on any work in this tree, and the shape-independent work in `docs/architecture/adr-0038-implementation-brief.md` §2.1 is the part a lane may take. |

| 40.6 | Public Website | REACHED | The landing application carries the six explanatory areas (`ls frontend/landing/app`), and §40.6's actual requirement — every quantitative statement carrying an architecture/target/demo/measured status — is now structural rather than authorial. **The mechanism.** `frontend/landing/components/elements/Claim.js` throws on a status outside the four and on a numeral kind outside its own, so a figure with an unchecked status cannot render; `frontend/landing/lib/claims.mjs` is the single definition of what counts as a quantity, and it reads spelled-out number words as well as digits, because most of this site's numbers spell out and a `\d`-only rule would have reported the site clean. **The test.** `frontend/landing/tests/claims.spec.mjs` walks the rendered DOM of every page — including the 404 body — and fails on any numeral with no annotated ancestor, quoting the sentence; beside it, an annotation must cover a quantity and must be statement-sized (so a `data-claim-status` on a wrapper cannot silence the sweep), and nothing may be labelled `measured` while `execution_nodes = {}` everywhere. `npx playwright test` in `frontend/landing` reports `72 passed`, `npm run lint` reports `landing lint: clean — 43 files, 12 routes checked`. **What it found on its first run**, which is the argument for it: the home page advertised `59` Rust crates against a workspace of 58 (`grep -c '"crates/' backend/Cargo.toml`); "Seven regional cells" was stated flat as an inventory when ADR 0035 calls seven "the blueprint's target and premature" and none is deployed, and it is now labelled `target` with a visible marker; and the three headline figures rendered as empty boxes until a scroll event that never fires on most pages, so the annotation over them was vacuous — the anti-blanket assertion caught that, not a person. **Two honest limits, stated rather than left to be found.** The detector does not read "one", "no" or "both" as quantities, because in English they are determiners far more often than counts and a rule whose output is mostly noise gets suppressed; a claim of exactly one can therefore reach a reader unannotated. And a quantity inside an `aria-label` or a `<meta name="description">` has no element to carry an attribute, so its status is declared in `DECLARED_ATTRIBUTE_CLAIMS` and `scripts/lint.mjs` holds the declaration to the source in both directions — undeclared strings and stale declarations both fail. |

| 41.3 | Thread and Core Assignment | PARTIAL | **None of the table's ten core groups, and the blocker is structural rather than unwritten — two of them, independently. Counted 2026-09-19** — `awk '/^41\.3 /{f=1} f&&/^41\.4 /{exit} f' docs/architecture/algorik-blueprint-v10.1-source.md` assigns named threads to cores 0 through 15 in ten groups. **First: the binary is single-threaded.** `grep -n 'thread::spawn' backend/crates/apps/qip-edge-node/src/main.rs` returns one line and it is below that file's only `#[cfg(test)]` (`grep -n '#\[cfg(test)\]' backend/crates/apps/qip-edge-node/src/main.rs`), so a deployed node spawns no thread at all — the health server renders on the same thread that flushes the journal, which is the deliberate design ADR 0001/0011 argue for. A table assigning sixteen threads to cores describes a process that does not exist here, and assigning zero of ten is the accurate reading rather than a harsh one. **Second, and it survives the first being fixed: nothing in this workspace may pin a thread.** `sched_setaffinity` is a raw syscall, which needs either `unsafe` — `grep -n 'unsafe_code' backend/Cargo.toml` forbids it at the workspace root — or a crate to wrap it, and the dependency policy permits two, neither of which is one (`./scripts/check-dependencies.sh`). So the row is not waiting on somebody to write it: closing it needs an ADR reversing `unsafe_code = "forbid"` or admitting a dependency, and it should be read the way §56.2's rule 22 is rather than as a backlog item. What exists is the *isolation*, and it is half of one row rather than half of the table. Half exists, in Terraform rather than in code: `grep -n 'isolated_cpus' infrastructure/terraform/modules/execution-node/main.tf` derives `2-(vcpus-1)` and the startup script refuses a kernel that lacks it (`grep -n 'isolcpus=' infrastructure/terraform/modules/execution-node/templates/startup.sh.tftpl`). The table itself — sixteen threads assigned to named cores — is absent from every binary: `grep -rn 'core_affinity\|sched_setaffinity' backend/crates --include=*.rs` returns nothing, and no thread in `qip-edge-node` is pinned. **Re-verified 2026-09-19**: every command in this cell re-run against the tree, all still hold and the verdict is unchanged. **The pinning table is retired as a non-goal by ADR 0082 (2026-09-19), not absent**, because pinning needs `unsafe` (`grep -n unsafe_code backend/Cargo.toml` → `forbid`, kept) or a crate the two-dependency rule forbids (kept), and because the node has one thread by design — `grep -n 'thread::spawn' backend/crates/apps/qip-edge-node/src/main.rs` prints one line, inside `mod tests`. If a thread is ever pinned the supervisor pins it (`CPUAffinity=` in the unit), decided in advance. A retired clause is not `REACHED`; the row stays `PARTIAL` on the Terraform half, with one consequence now written down: `isolcpus` with no affinity call schedules the process onto cores 0–1, so a provisioned node would run its one thread beside the OS while the isolated cores idle — the unit-file remedy landed later on 2026-09-19 as a plan-proven change under ADR 0069: `qip-execution-node.service` carries `CPUAffinity=${first_isolated_cpu}` (`grep -n 'CPUAffinity' infrastructure/terraform/modules/execution-node/templates/startup.sh.tftpl`), the core is derived beside the range in `main.tf` (`grep -n 'first_isolated_cpu' infrastructure/terraform/modules/execution-node/main.tf`), and `terraform test` in `modules/execution-node` plans an eight-vCPU shape and reads `CPUAffinity=2` off the rendered instance-template metadata while refusing the four-vCPU shape (`infrastructure/terraform/modules/execution-node/tests/affinity.tftest.hcl`, 2 runs; mutation: the line deleted → the admitting run fails naming the pin). So the `isolcpus` refusal now guards a core the process actually runs on. The table's ten groups are still retired, not met, and the verdict does not move. Reversal is a deployed node's scraped series showing preemption, which cannot fire before something scrapes. |

| 43.1 | World and Cognition | REACHED | **14 of 14, and the gap was never the name the last three revisions of this cell argued about — it was that nothing asserted the object. Flipped 2026-09-19.** This cell had reasoned its way to the right conclusion about `WorldEvent` (the graph holds it; minting a struct beside it would be a second source of truth) and then stopped, leaving the row `PARTIAL` on a naming question §43's preamble raises about all four of its subsections equally. The register's bar is built, tested, and a non-test call path; `WorldEvent` failed only the middle one. `grep -rn 'NodeKind::Event\|RelationshipKind::ConcernsEntity' --include=*.rs backend/crates | grep -v '/tests/'` prints **two** lines, both in `qip-world-model/src/world.rs`'s `absorb_news` — it is the sole writer of an occurrence and of the link to the entity it concerns — and before 2026-09-19 the same command restricted to `/tests/` printed **nothing at all**. The existing news test asserts entity resolution, document retrieval and sentiment and walks straight past the occurrence. `an_absorbed_news_item_becomes_an_occurrence_carrying_its_source_and_both_instants` in `backend/crates/services/qip-world-model/tests/understanding.rs` now drives every part of the blueprint's definition — "an observed occurrence linked to entities, with source, event time, receipt time and confidence" — and publishes three days before it absorbs, so an implementation stamping `now` into both instants fails rather than passes. Four mutations fired on property assertions: event time stamped at absorption, the `source` attribute dropped, the occurrence filed as `Evidence`, and the link given a fixed weight instead of the mention's confidence. `cargo test -p qip-world-model --test understanding` — 55 passed, 0 failed. The production path is unchanged and is the `SensedRecord::News` arm of `Platform::observe` (`grep -rn 'absorb_news' --include=*.rs backend/crates | grep -v '/tests/'`). `EntityRelation` is `Relationship`/`RelationshipKind` (`grep -n 'pub struct Relationship\|pub enum RelationshipKind' backend/crates/services/qip-world-model/src/relationship.rs`) — a name grep for the blueprint's spelling is not a search for the object, and this cell has now made that error twice and corrected it twice. The other twelve: `grep -rn 'pub struct CausalEdge\|pub struct CausalGraph' backend/crates/services/qip-world-model/src/causal.rs`, `grep -rn 'pub struct Episode' backend/crates/libs/qip-agents/src/memory.rs`, `grep -rn 'pub struct CapabilityEstimate' backend/crates/services/qip-learning-engine/src/self_model.rs`, `grep -rn 'pub struct SourceCandidate' backend/crates/services/qip-data-finder/src/source.rs`, `grep -rn 'pub struct DeepWebAdapter' backend/crates/services/qip-data-finder/src/tier.rs`. **Two limits stated rather than buried.** First, the check that all fourteen are tested is coarse — each object's name appears in at least one file under a crate's `tests/`, counted on 2026-09-19, which is weaker than reading each assertion; the fourteenth was read line by line because it was the one at zero. Second, the only production writer of an occurrence is the news arm, so every `WorldEvent` in the graph is a news item: filings, chain updates and corporate actions write features and market events instead. The blueprint's definition names no source and does not require one, but a reader expecting a filing to be an occurrence will not find one. §43's own framing — "all defined once in the types crate" — remains unmet, and it is unmet identically by §43.2, §43.3 and §43.4; it is an observation about the whole section and cannot single this subsection out. |

`AssetValuation` (which carries `ValuationMethod` and `ValuationInput`) and `CorporateAction` from `grep -n 'illiquid_marks\|corporate_actions_pending' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs`; `Commitment` from `grep -n 'CommitmentBook::new\|Commitment::from_private_asset' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs`; `AuctionState` from `grep -n 'AuctionState' backend/crates/edge/qip-orderbook/src/venue.rs`; and `CapitalCall` through `Commitment::obligation`, which `CommitmentBook::unfunded_total` sums and which the kernel subtracts from free capital before anything is sized (`grep -n 'unfunded_total' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs` returned four lines, three of them calls and the fourth a comment inside the `#[cfg(test)]` module — read them, do not count them). **The two that are read by nothing are why this stays PARTIAL, and neither is an oversight.** `VolatilitySurface` is deliberately without a caller: ADR 0050 settles that nothing may feed it until an option-quote source passes the licensing gate, and explicitly rejects fitting it to the platform's own numbers (`grep -n 'Synthetic\|alternatives' docs/adr/0050-what-an-option-quote-source-must-satisfy-before-the-volatility-surface-has-a-caller.md`). Do not wire it to close this row. **`CashflowForecast` is the one that moved, and the claim that it "is constructed only in tests" was false when it was written here.** The cell reasoned from `with_forecast` having no production caller, which is still true and is a different question from whether a forecast is *built*. It is: `grep -rn 'forecast_private_asset' backend/crates --include=*.rs \| grep -v /tests/` returns the definition in `qip-financial/src/valuation.rs`, its use inside that engine, and a call in `qip-kernel/src/platform.rs` from `private_holdings_of`, which builds one for every private asset in the universe at assembly and reads `first_return_at` off it into the liquidity ladder's `days_to_exit`. The reader has a consequence: before it, the only thing the risk read knew about how fast a private fund becomes cash was the catalogue's estimate, and a catalogue cannot know a lockup. What remains unreached is specifically `Commitment::with_forecast`, and since 2026-09-18 the type *refuses* the one forecast the platform builds (`grep -n 'calls == 0 && unfunded.is_positive' backend/crates/libs/qip-financial/src/cashflow.rs`), because that forecast holds a distribution and no call — attaching it would read silence about calls as "no call, ever" and take the reserve on a wholly unfunded commitment to nothing. That is a refusal working, not a gap; closing it needs a call schedule an administrator published, not a caller. The superseded evidence, for the record: `Commitment::from_private_asset` goes through `unscheduled`, which sets the forecast to `None`, and `grep -rn '\.with_forecast(' backend/crates --include=*.rs \| grep -v /tests/` returns only an unrelated `DemandForecast` builder in the pre-positioner and a doc comment, so no production commitment carries a pacing model. `LandedCost` has no reader at all — `grep -rn 'LandedCost\|PhysicalArbitrage' backend/crates --include=*.rs \| grep -v qip-financial` still returns nothing, re-run 2026-09-19 — and **this is settled rather than open, which this cell did not say.** ADR 0072 decides that `qip_financial::physical` stays without a caller and writes its own reversal condition by name: "A production caller for `qip_financial::physical` — if an ingestion path for carrier tariffs or a customs schedule lands" (`grep -n 'A production caller for' docs/adr/0072-a-collateral-graph-refuses-the-arrangements-it-cannot-cost-and-a-physical-rate-carries-its-source.md`). None has landed. The module's own header says the same and says why: `LogisticsTerms` has no `Default` and `Spoilage` refuses a rate that does not name its source, because a default freight rate would be a number this repository invented and then quoted back as though it had measured it. **Do not give it a caller to close this row** — that is ADR 0050's prohibition in a second place. **`CapitalCall`'s read path reaches production and its write path does not, and re-checking that on 2026-09-19 found the read is hollow rather than merely partial.** `Commitment::obligation` is `unfunded() + accrued_default_penalty()`, and the penalty term iterates `self.calls`, a map only `Commitment::record_call` inserts into: `grep -rn '\.record_call(\|CapitalCall::notice' backend/crates --include=*.rs` returns one internal delegation inside `cashflow.rs` — `CommitmentBook::record_call` handing to `Commitment::record_call` — and otherwise nothing but `qip-financial/tests/cashflow.rs`. So the term was structurally zero on every cycle that had ever run, and `unfunded_total` and `obligation` returned the same number by construction rather than by coincidence — until the writer at the end of this row landed the same evening; the finding is kept because it is why the writer had to reach the reserve and not only the book. **Closing it is an ADR, not a route, and the decisive reason is not the one §16.4 gives.** §16.4 says "a data-model change, not a wiring change" and it is right that `PrivateAssetDetails` cannot supply a notice — `grep -n 'pub struct PrivateAssetDetails' -A 12 backend/crates/libs/qip-financial/src/extensions.rs` carries committed, called, distributed and residual capital and a notice *period*, and no reference, no outstanding amount and no due date, while `CapitalCall::notice` requires the fund's own identifier and argues in its own doc why it must not be generated. But the harder obstacle is lifetime: the commitment book is derived state, rebuilt from the `Universe` at construction and never reloaded — `grep -n 'private_holdings_of' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs` returns the definition and exactly one call, inside the constructor. A notice filed through an operator intake on `Platform` would therefore be **erased at the next process start**, taking the reserve it raised down with it and leaving no record that it had ever been higher. An intake is safe in direction — `record_call` never touches `unfunded()`, so a notice can only ever *raise* the obligation — and that is not enough: what must be decided first is where a filed notice lives so that a replay reproduces it, who may file one and what authenticates them, and how a notice is amended when `record_call` deliberately refuses a duplicate reference. Those are the questions an ADR answers. The kernel half would be Lane N1's (`Platform` exposes only `commitments()`, read-only — `grep -nE 'pub (const )?fn [a-z_]*commitment' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs` returns one line), and a `qip-api` route written before that decision would be a second source of truth for a fact the event log should hold. **`CapitalCall`'s writer: decided 2026-09-19 by lane D1 in ADR 0085 §5, and built the same evening by lane X1 on that design, with a production call path that is refused in fact until Gate A — so the row moves within `PARTIAL` and not out of it, and the paragraph after this one says what was built, what reads it, and what still holds the verdict.** The three questions D1 answered were these: the three questions above — where a filed notice lives so a replay reproduces it, who files it and what authenticates them, and how a duplicate reference is handled — are answered. A notice is **not** the same event family as a user's expected inflow, and the brief that proposed it had the direction backwards: a call is a drawdown demand *on* the desk's commitment (`grep -n 'A drawdown demand' backend/crates/libs/qip-financial/src/cashflow.rs`), an obligation the reserve subtracts, where an inflow is a user's deposit into their own book. It shares the *discipline* ADR 0085 §1 sets for the inflow — a journalled declaration under an authenticated operator's subject, reference-idempotent through the type's own refusal (`Commitment::record_call` already refuses a duplicate), `CallConsequence` carried on the record, replayed at boot into the commitment book `private_holdings_of` has just built, and refused closed when the commitment the notice names is no longer held — as its **own** record type beside the commitment book, not inside `LedgerEntry`. The writer needed three things; two of the three are now built and the third is the one this repository cannot issue. **What was built (X1, 2026-09-19).** The record family is its own, beside the ledger and not inside it: `grep -n 'pub enum CapitalCallEntry\|pub struct CapitalCallNotice\|const CAPITAL_CALL_ORIGIN' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs` — `Noticed` and `Withdrawn`, journalled under the producer `kernel/capital-call`, each carrying the `CallConsequence` and the operator's subject from the identity and never from a body. The seams: `grep -n 'pub fn record_capital_call\|pub fn withdraw_capital_call\|fn resume_capital_calls' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs` — the filing checks the book's own gates on a scratch copy, journals, then applies, in the order `expect_inflow` keeps; the withdrawal is the amendment path, since `record_call` refuses a duplicate reference by design; and the resume replays both in log order into the book `private_holdings_of` has just built and stops assembly, naming the record and the remedy, when this boot's universe will not take one (`grep -n 'fn resume_' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs` now names five seams in this file where it named four that morning, and the one directly after `resume_ledger` is the commitment book's — run it rather than counting this sentence). `Commitment::withdraw_call` and `CommitmentBook::withdraw_call` were added to `cashflow.rs` for it, additively. **What reads it, which is the bar.** `grep -rn '\.record_call(\|CapitalCall::notice' backend/crates --include=*.rs \| grep -v /tests/` now returns the internal delegation in `cashflow.rs` and four sites in `platform.rs` — the filing and the resume — where on the morning of 2026-09-19 it returned the delegation alone. The reserve reads a filed notice through the path that already existed and was hollow: `grep -n 'commitments.unfunded_total' backend/crates/runtime/qip-kernel/src/platform.rs` returns `arm_horizon_gate` and `deployable_capital`, and `qip-kernel/tests/capital_calls.rs::an_overdue_capital_call_shrinks_the_capital_the_platform_will_deploy_by_what_missing_it_cost` proves a notice ten days overdue at 3,650bp takes exactly its 1,000 penalty off `deployable_capital` and a notice ahead of its date takes nothing — the arithmetic's own promise, and the reason a filed notice can only ever *raise* the reserve. The routes: `grep -n 'pattern: "/ledger/commitments' backend/crates/apps/qip-api/src/routes.rs` — `POST /ledger/commitments/:commitment/capital-calls` and its `DELETE` by reference at operator, in `api_boundary.rs`'s mutating set and typed-intent allowlist and in `api.rs`'s presence-gate table, and `GET /ledger/commitments` at viewer rendering each commitment as the reserve reads it beside `CALL_SETTLEMENT`; the contract is in `qip-api/ROUTES-LEDGER.md`. **What still holds this row at `PARTIAL`, in order of weight.** (1) The operator routes are authorised in shape and refused in fact: every credential the API accepts is a standing bearer token and `Principal::authentication_instant` refuses one, so the production call path reaches `Platform::record_capital_call` from a route and reaches no deployed process's *act* until the per-person credential of ADR 0076 exists — the same half-state the inflow routes of §40.12 are in, and stated as such rather than as reached. (2) **Settlement is deliberately not built, and this is a decision rather than a gap**: `grep -rn 'settle_call' backend/crates --include=*.rs \| grep -v /tests/` returns its definition in `cashflow.rs` and one doc-comment mention and no caller. Meeting a call is capital leaving the desk, and the only party that can attest it is the custodian the reserve was paid from; the one statement the platform observes is the desk's own wallet, which names venues and assets and never a fund administrator (`grep -rn 'CapitalCallFromReserve' backend/crates --include=*.rs \| grep -v /tests/` returns the corridor *kind* in `qip-capital-fabric/src/custody.rs`, which its own doc says "nothing in this crate can enter"). An operator settling a call would lower the reserve on a person's claim about the bank's fact — ADR 0085 §2's refusal, in the direction that loosens sizing. So the reserve can be *reported against* a call and never *drawn against* one here. The reversal condition is the same as §2's: a custodian statement line at the fund administrator naming the notice reference and the amount, observed through the mounted-file path the wallet statement takes, gives `settle_call` its caller in the statement's observation path — not in a route — and deletes `CALL_SETTLEMENT`. (3) The fail-closed cost of the resume is real and named in its doc: a catalogue update that re-dates a commitment past a standing notice, or reduces its unfunded balance below the notices standing, stops assembly; the remedy is to withdraw the notice on the running process before deploying that universe, or archive the log. (4) Unchanged from the paragraphs above: `VolatilitySurface` and `LandedCost` stay without a caller by ADR 0050 and ADR 0072, and `Commitment::with_forecast` stays unreached because the type refuses the one forecast the platform builds. |
